=== BUGGY CODE ===
# Definition for a binary tree node.
# class TreeNode:
#     def __init__(self, val=0, left=None, right=None):
#         self.val = val
#         self.left = left
#         self.right = right
class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue=deque()
        queue.append(root)
        lst=[]
        while queue:
            levels=[]
            for i in range(len(queue)+1): 
                tmp=queue.popleft()
                if tmp:
                    levels.append(tmp.val)
                    queue.append(tmp.right)  
                    queue.append(tmp.left)   

            if levels:
                lst.add(levels)
        return lst[::-1]

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

```python
from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue = deque([root])
        lst = []
        while queue:
            levels = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                node = queue.popleft()
                if node:
                    levels.append(node.val)
                    queue.append(node.left)
                    queue.append(node.right)
            if levels:
                lst.append(levels)
        return lst[::-1]
```

Let's ensure code style:
```
from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        queue = deque([root])
        result = []
        while queue:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                node = queue.popleft()
                if node:
                    level.append(node.val)
                    queue.append(node.left)
                    queue.append(node.right)
            if level:
                result.append(level)
        return result[::-1]
```
This is clean.

Let's decide final code. I'd prefer:
```
from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []
        queue = deque([root])
        result = []
        while queue:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                node = queue.popleft()
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    queue.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    queue.append(node.right)
            result.append(level)
        return result[::-1]
```
This is more efficient and avoids None. But is it "fixed code" from original? It changes more than necessary but still correct. The original appended None; this doesn't. Both accepted. from collections import deque
```python
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []

        queue = deque([root])
        result = []

        while queue:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                node = queue.popleft()
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    queue.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    queue.append(node.right)
            result.append(level)

        return result[::-1]
```

from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []

        queue = deque([root])
        result = []

        while queue:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                node = queue.popleft()
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    queue.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    queue.append(node.right)
            result.append(level)

        return result[::-1]

=== RAW ANSWER ===
from collections import deque
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []

        queue = deque([root])
        result = []

        while queue:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(queue)):
                node = queue.popleft()
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    queue.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    queue.append(node.right)
            result.append(level)

        return result[::-1]
